\section{The signed source norm and the original zeta counting
measure}\label{the-signed-source-norm-and-the-original-zeta-counting-measure}

24 September 2026. Exact comparison PRZ1--PRZ4. The user's \(Z_2\)
denotes the retained parity observation; this note does not redefine the
user's entire \(Z_n\) hierarchy as a family of groups. No addition at
tau is used.

\subsection{PRZ1. The source operation actually supplying
parity}\label{prz1.-the-source-operation-actually-supplying-parity}

The signed generic monoid in the author's \texttt{CC.tex},
\href{https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.00299v1}{Connes--Consani,
arXiv:2609.00299v1}, has nonabsorbing group \[
G=\{T^mJ^k:m\in\mathbb Z,\ k\in\mathbb Z/4\mathbb Z\},
\quad J^2=\epsilon,\quad\epsilon^2=1,
\] and involution \[
A(T)=\epsilon T^{-1},\qquad A(J)=\epsilon J=J^{-1}.
\tag{PRZ1.1}
\] Here the cyclic four-element factor is the author's quarter-phase
structure, not an imposed replacement for the user's quantum-number
notation. The full source, its absorbing element and the supporting
point remain separate objects. Multiplying the two source elements gives
\[
A(T^mJ^k)=T^{-m}J^{2m-k},\qquad
(T^mJ^k)A(T^mJ^k)=J^{2m}=\epsilon^m.
\tag{PRZ1.2}
\] Thus the norm observation is independent of the quarter phase and
descends to winding \(L=G/\langle J\rangle\). It records even and odd
winding while retaining the full original element alongside the
observation. This is the earlier
\href{https://github.com/KokunoYumeto/zeta-function-research-reader/blob/6bc7202eceb05d1a482d495ad302803a80b5aeca/workbenches/splitzero-tandem/temporary-arguments/20260924-fixed-generic-point-purity/prime-clocks-p14/WINDING_PARITY_AND_INTEGER_SPECTRUM.md\#L55-L85}{W2
proof}, reproduced here to prove the exact source of the following
labels.

The complete chart construction recovers arithmetic endomorphisms
\([n]:L\to L\); evaluation on the positive chart's intrinsic generator
identifies the positive count \(n\) with the winding class of \(T^n\).
Reversing the chart changes \(n\) to \(-n\), which has the same norm
\(\epsilon^n\). No exchanged parity label is thereby assigned to the
fixed supporting point \(\tau\).

\subsection{PRZ2. Retaining the two parity components, the unit, and all
four
lifts}\label{prz2.-retaining-the-two-parity-components-the-unit-and-all-four-lifts}

For the original count measure
\(\mathcal D=\sum_{n\ge1}\delta_{\log n}\), define \[
\mathcal D_{\rm even}=\sum_{m\ge1}\delta_{\log(2m)},\qquad
\mathcal D_{\rm odd}=\sum_{m\ge0}\delta_{\log(2m+1)}.
\tag{PRZ2.1}
\] These are the two fibres of PRZ1.2 on positive counts. The
integer-one atom \(\delta_0\) belongs to the odd component. It is
neither integer zero nor tau. The maps are exactly \[
\mathcal D_{\rm even}=\delta_{\log2}*\mathcal D,
\qquad\mathcal D_{\rm odd}=\mathcal D-\mathcal D_{\rm even},
\qquad\mathcal D=\mathcal D_{\rm even}+\mathcal D_{\rm odd}.
\tag{PRZ2.2}
\] The first identity follows from \(\log(2m)=\log2+\log m\). The second
is a disjoint partition of the positive counts, so both measures are
positive despite the displayed subtraction. The ordered pair is
retained, rather than only its sum.

One may also retain every source lift explicitly as \[
\widetilde{\mathcal D}
=\sum_{n\ge1}\sum_{k=0}^{3}\delta_{(\log n,T^nJ^k)}.
\tag{PRZ2.3}
\] For the map \(\pi(t,g)=t\), the exact pushforwards are
\(\pi_*\widetilde{\mathcal D}=4\mathcal D\) and, on each parity
component, \(4\mathcal D_{\rm even}\) or \(4\mathcal D_{\rm odd}\). The
factor four has not been absorbed. The arithmetic count measure counts
winding classes once; the full lift measure counts the four
representatives once each. These are the stated map and its
multiplicity, not an identification of the two measures.

\subsection{PRZ3. A faithful meromorphic receiver of the original
divisor}\label{prz3.-a-faithful-meromorphic-receiver-of-the-original-divisor}

On \(\Re s>1\), absolute convergence permits the partition of the
original series, giving \[
E(s)=\mathcal L\mathcal D_{\rm even}(s)=2^{-s}\zeta(s),\qquad
O(s)=\mathcal L\mathcal D_{\rm odd}(s)=(1-2^{-s})\zeta(s).
\tag{PRZ3.1}
\] The ordered receiver \(\zeta\mapsto(E,O)\) has the explicit inverse
\((E,O)\mapsto E+O\). More strongly, \(2^{-s}=\exp(-s\log2)\) is entire
and nowhere zero, with entire inverse \(2^s\). Therefore \(\zeta=2^sE\)
is a global equality of meromorphic functions, and \[
\operatorname{ord}_{s_0}E=\operatorname{ord}_{s_0}\zeta
\quad\text{at every finite }s_0.
\tag{PRZ3.2}
\] Every original zero, multiplicity, and pole is retained. The odd
component has the additional explicitly recorded divisor \[
\operatorname{ord}_{s_0}O
=\operatorname{ord}_{s_0}\zeta+\operatorname{ord}_{s_0}(1-2^{-s}).
\tag{PRZ3.3}
\] The extra factor's zeros are \(s_0=2\pi i k/\log2\),
\(k\in\mathbb Z\), each simple because its derivative there is
\(\log2\ne0\). In particular no zero or pole of the original zeta is
silently deleted by using the ordered pair.

\subsection{PRZ4. What the signed trace does to the
endpoint}\label{prz4.-what-the-signed-trace-does-to-the-endpoint}

Evaluating the norm sign by \(\epsilon\mapsto-1\) gives the signed
scalar \[
S(s)=E(s)-O(s)=(2^{1-s}-1)\zeta(s)
=\sum_{n\ge1}(-1)^n n^{-s}\quad(\Re s>1).
\tag{PRZ4.1}
\] This is the negative of the usual alternating Dirichlet eta function.
The letter \(S\) avoids conflating it with the source's generic point
\(\eta\). The cancellation can be traced without losing either parity
component. The paired series \[
S(s)=\sum_{m\ge1}\bigl((2m)^{-s}-(2m-1)^{-s}\bigr)
=-s\sum_{m\ge1}\int_{2m-1}^{2m}x^{-s-1}\,dx
\tag{PRZ4.2}
\] converges absolutely and locally uniformly on \(\Re s>0\): on a
compact set there, its terms are bounded by a constant times
\((2m-1)^{-1-\delta}\) for some \(\delta>0\). It agrees with PRZ4.1 on
the original convergence half-plane, hence gives its analytic
continuation to this larger domain.

Put \(c(s)=2^{1-s}-1\). It has simple zeros at \(1+2\pi i k/\log2\).
Globally the exact meromorphic return is \(\zeta=S/c\), with Laurent
division at these points. At \(s=1+h\), the original pole and the
retained multiplier give \[
c(1+h)=e^{-h\log2}-1=-h\log2+O(h^2),\qquad
\zeta(1+h)=h^{-1}+O(1),\qquad S(1)=-\log2.
\tag{PRZ4.3}
\] The pole has cancelled in this signed scalar, while remaining in
\(E\), \(O\), their sum, and the displayed inverse. At every finite
point the full order relation is
\(\operatorname{ord}S=\operatorname{ord}c+\operatorname{ord}\zeta\).
Inside \(0<\Re s<1\), \(c\ne0\), so this signed observation preserves
all original zeros and their multiplicities there. The source norm
supplies an actual parity-resolved zeta map; none of these identities
determines their real parts.

\section{An integral adjoint algebra for the complete positive counting
action}\label{an-integral-adjoint-algebra-for-the-complete-positive-counting-action}

24 September 2026. Independent derivation. Proof locators IAR0--IAR9.

This note constructs the smallest unital subring of the original
rational group algebra that contains every positive counting operator
and is preserved by the actual weighted adjoint. It retains the original
rational group algebra as an ambient object and recovers it by scalar
extension. No quotient replaces it. The supporting datum remains
\(\tau\langle Z_1;\text{no intrinsic }Z_2\rangle\); no addition or
arithmetic value is assigned to tau.

\subsection{IAR0. Input and exact scope of the
correction}\label{iar0.-input-and-exact-scope-of-the-correction}

Read
\nolinkurl{work/tau_weight_cohomology_20260924/INTEGRAL_COUNTING_AND_WEIGHT_COMPARISON.md},
IC1--IC9, particularly the complete IC3 and IC8 arguments.

Retain the original rational group algebra

\[
K=\mathbb Q[\mathbb Q_{>0}^{\times}]
=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb Q_{>0}^{\times}}\mathbb Q b_r,
\qquad b_rb_s=b_{rs},\qquad 1_K=b_1,
\tag{IAR0.1}
\]

with the specified involution

\[
b_r^*=r b_{1/r},\qquad c^*=c\quad(c\in\mathbb Q).
\tag{IAR0.2}
\]

All sums have finite support. The multiplication is commutative, so its
involution can equivalently be described as multiplicative or
antimultiplicative. Involution follows directly from
\((r b_{1/r})^*=r(1/r)b_r\).

IC8 proves that imposing this involution on a ring already containing
every bare \(b_{1/n}\) forces rational scalars. That theorem is correct.
Here the input is the actual positive counting monoid \(\{b_n:n\ge1\}\);
its inverses are not assumed to be integral. The resulting ring is

\[
\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}
=\mathbb Z[b_n,n b_{1/n}:n\ge1]\subseteq K.
\tag{IAR0.3}
\]

The word integral here refers to an explicit free \(\mathbb Z\)-form of
the algebra. It does not mean that its generators are integral over
\(\mathbb Z\), and it does not assert a finite-rank integral lattice for
a complex zero block.

\subsection{IAR1. A complete additive basis, including every
denominator}\label{iar1.-a-complete-additive-basis-including-every-denominator}

Write a positive rational uniquely in lowest terms as \(r=a/b\), where
\(a,b\ge1\) and \(\gcd(a,b)=1\). Set

\[
\operatorname{den}(r)=b,\quad\operatorname{num}(r)=a,
\qquad C_r=b b_r.
\tag{IAR1.1}
\]

\textbf{Theorem.} The elements \(C_r\), for all positive rationals
\(r\), form a free additive \(\mathbb Z\)-basis of
\(\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\). Their exact multiplication and involution
are

\[
\boxed{
C_rC_s=
\frac{\operatorname{den}(r)\operatorname{den}(s)}
{\operatorname{den}(rs)}C_{rs},
\qquad C_r^*=C_{1/r}.}
\tag{IAR1.2}
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Put \(r=a/b\), \(s=c/d\) in lowest terms and let
\(g=\gcd(ac,bd)\). Then

\[
rs=\frac{ac/g}{bd/g},\qquad
\operatorname{den}(rs)=bd/g,
\]

so the coefficient in (IAR1.2) is the positive integer \(g\). In the
original basis,

\[
C_rC_s=bd\,b_{rs}
=\frac{bd}{bd/g}\,C_{rs}=g C_{rs}.
\]

Every denominator factor has therefore been included. Also

\[
C_r^*=b\frac ab b_{b/a}=a b_{b/a}=C_{1/r}.
\]

The additive span \(L=\bigoplus_r\mathbb Z C_r\) is a unital subring,
since \(C_1=b_1\), and is preserved by \(*\). It contains \(b_n=C_n\)
and \(n b_{1/n}=C_{1/n}\), so \(\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\subseteq L\).
Conversely,

\[
b_a\,(b b_{1/b})=b b_{a/b}=C_{a/b},
\]

which puts every basis element of \(L\) in \(\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\).
Finally, any finite integral relation \(\sum_r m_r C_r=0\) has
\(b_r\)-coefficient \(m_r\operatorname{den}(r)\); freeness of the
original rational group basis forces every \(m_r=0\). Thus the span is
free and equals the claimed ring. \(\square\)

This proves the exact chain of inclusions

\[
\mathbb Z[b_n:n\ge1]
\subseteq\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}
\subseteq\Lambda:=\mathbb Z[\mathbb Q_{>0}^{\times}]
\subseteq K.
\tag{IAR1.3}
\]

For \(n>1\), the bare basis vector \(b_{1/n}\) does not lie in
\(\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\): its coefficient one is not a multiple of the
required denominator \(n\). Also

\[
\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\cap\mathbb Q b_1=\mathbb Z b_1.
\tag{IAR1.4}
\]

Thus stability under this adjoint has not introduced any nonintegral
scalar.

\subsection{IAR2. Minimality and rational scalar
extension}\label{iar2.-minimality-and-rational-scalar-extension}

\textbf{Theorem.} \(\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\) is the unique smallest
unital subring of \(K\) that contains all \(b_n\), \(n\ge1\), and is
preserved by \(*\).

\textbf{Proof.} A unital subring contains the integral scalar multiples
of its unit. If it contains \(b_n\), involution stability also gives
\(b_n^*=n b_{1/n}\). It therefore contains every generator in (IAR0.3),
hence the entire displayed ring. Conversely IAR1 proves that this ring
is involution-stable and contains the required counting elements.
\(\square\)

Scalar extension is an isomorphism onto the original ambient algebra:

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb Q\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}
\xrightarrow{\sim}K,\qquad q\otimes C_r\longmapsto
q\operatorname{den}(r)b_r.}
\tag{IAR2.1}
\]

Surjectivity follows from \(b_r=\operatorname{den}(r)^{-1}C_r\);
injectivity follows from the same free-basis argument as IAR1. This map
preserves products, units and involutions by their explicit formulas.

There is also an exact additive record of the difference from the bare
integral group ring:

\[
\Lambda/\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}
\cong\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb Q_{>0}^{\times}}
\mathbb Z/\operatorname{den}(r)\mathbb Z.
\tag{IAR2.2}
\]

This is an additive quotient, not a quotient ring:
\(\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\) contains the unit and is not an ideal of
\(\Lambda\). Formula (IAR2.2) follows coordinate by coordinate from
(IAR1.1). In particular the discrepancy is torsion, exactly as the
scalar-extension isomorphism records.

\subsection{IAR3. The prime-by-prime
presentation}\label{iar3.-the-prime-by-prime-presentation}

For every prime \(p\), define in the original ambient ring

\[
F_p=b_p,\qquad V_p=p b_{1/p}.
\tag{IAR3.1}
\]

They obey

\[
F_pV_p=V_pF_p=p b_1,
\quad F_p^*=V_p,\quad V_p^*=F_p.
\tag{IAR3.2}
\]

Elements associated to distinct primes commute. The letter \(V\) denotes
this particular generator and is not an assertion identifying it with a
Frobenius or Verschiebung functor from another theory.

\textbf{Theorem.} There is a unital, involution-preserving isomorphism

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\cong
\bigotimes_{p\text{ prime},\,\mathbb Z}
\mathbb Z[F_p,V_p]/(F_pV_p-p).}
\tag{IAR3.3}
\]

The infinite tensor product means the filtered union of its finite-prime
tensor products under their unital inclusions. Equivalently its
presentation is the commutative polynomial ring in all displayed
variables, divided by the ideal generated by all \(F_pV_p-p\).

\textbf{Proof.} In each monomial, replace a simultaneous factor
\(F_p^aV_p^b\) by

\[
p^{\min(a,b)}
\begin{cases}
F_p^{a-b},&a\ge b,\\
V_p^{b-a},&b\ge a.
\end{cases}
\tag{IAR3.4}
\]

Thus every polynomial is an integral linear combination of monomials
that use, for each prime, only a power of \(F_p\), only a power of
\(V_p\), or neither. The monomial indexed by finitely supported integers
\((e_p)_p\) is

\[
M_e=\prod_{e_p>0}F_p^{e_p}
\prod_{e_p<0}V_p^{-e_p}.
\]

Its image in \(K\) is

\[
\left(\prod_{e_p<0}p^{-e_p}\right)b_{\prod_p p^{e_p}}
=C_{\prod_p p^{e_p}}.
\tag{IAR3.5}
\]

Unique prime factorization makes these rational indices distinct for
distinct exponent tuples. IAR1 proves linear independence of their
images. Hence the reduction has no further relation, the presented ring
embeds in \(K\), and its image is the entire basis span
\(\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\). Swapping \(F_p,V_p\) preserves each relation
and agrees with the computed involution. This proves every assertion.
\(\square\)

In particular the ring is a domain: any two elements of its ambient
algebra belong to a Laurent polynomial ring over \(\mathbb Q\) on a
finite set of prime variables, which is a domain. It is also free, hence
flat, as a \(\mathbb Z\)-module; tensoring its direct sum of copies of
\(\mathbb Z\) preserves every injection. No finiteness over
\(\mathbb Z\) is claimed: already the powers of a single \(F_p\) are
linearly independent. In fact \(F_p\) satisfies no monic polynomial over
\(\mathbb Z\), since such a polynomial would be a relation among the
distinct basis elements \(C_{p^j}\). The integral adjoint algebra
therefore does not itself make every represented eigenvalue an algebraic
integer.

\subsection{IAR4. How the earlier rationalization obstruction fits
exactly}\label{iar4.-how-the-earlier-rationalization-obstruction-fits-exactly}

Localizing the integral adjoint algebra at all positive generators gives
the previously used bare integral group ring:

\[
\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}[F_p^{-1}:p\text{ prime}]
\cong\mathbb Z[b_p,b_p^{-1}:p\text{ prime}]=\Lambda.
\tag{IAR4.1}
\]

Indeed \(F_p^{-1}=b_{1/p}\), and all generators in either direction are
then present, with the same injection into \(K\). This localization is
not stable under the weighted involution over its integral scalars,
precisely as IC8 proves.

Inverting both adjoint partners gives

\[
\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}[F_p^{-1},V_p^{-1}:p\text{ prime}]
\cong K.
\tag{IAR4.2}
\]

To see the rational scalars explicitly, \(p=F_pV_p\) becomes a unit for
every prime, so \(1/p=F_p^{-1}V_p^{-1}\) belongs to this localization.
It also contains all group basis elements and hence every finite
rational combination. Conversely all these inverses already exist in
\(K\).

Thus the IC8 obstruction is retained, with its exact input identified.
It concerns adjoining the bare inverse operators as integral elements
and then imposing the adjoint. Starting from the actual positive
counting operators and adjoining their adjoints instead produces the
smaller involution-stable integral form proved above. The connecting
localizations (IAR4.1)--(IAR4.2) give their complete relation.

\subsection{IAR5. Every prime special fibre, with both components
retained}\label{iar5.-every-prime-special-fibre-with-both-components-retained}

Fix any prime \(\ell\) and write \(k=\mathbb F_\ell\). Let

\[
B_\ell=k[U_p,U_p^{-1}:p\text{ prime},\ p\ne\ell].
\tag{IAR5.1}
\]

Then base change of the proved presentation yields

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\otimes_{\mathbb Z}k
\cong B_\ell[F,V]/(FV),}
\quad F=F_\ell\bmod\ell,\quad V=V_\ell\bmod\ell.
\tag{IAR5.2}
\]

\textbf{Proof.} For \(p\ne\ell\), the relation is \(F_pV_p=\bar p\ne0\).
Thus \(F_p\) is a unit and \(V_p=\bar p F_p^{-1}\); this factor is
exactly the Laurent polynomial ring with coordinate \(U_p=F_p\). At
\(p=\ell\) the relation becomes \(FV=0\). Polynomial presentations
commute with scalar base change, proving (IAR5.2). Alternatively the
free integral basis in IAR3 reduces directly to its stated basis over
\(k\). \(\square\)

This fibre is reduced and has exactly two minimal prime ideals, \((F)\)
and \((V)\). Its two components are \(\operatorname{Spec}B_\ell[V]\) and
\(\operatorname{Spec}B_\ell[F]\), intersecting in
\(\operatorname{Spec}B_\ell\), the locus \(F=V=0\). Here is a proof that
includes all scheme-theoretic information in these claims. The map

\[
B_\ell[F,V]/(FV)\longrightarrow B_\ell[F]\times B_\ell[V],
\quad q\longmapsto(q(F,0),q(0,V))
\tag{IAR5.3}
\]

is injective, since each class has a unique form consisting of a
constant, positive powers of \(F\), and positive powers of \(V\). Its
image is precisely the pairs with equal constant term. Each factor is a
domain, so the fibre has no nonzero nilpotents. Every prime ideal
contains \(F\) or \(V\), since their product is zero, and the quotients
by either displayed ideal are domains. Neither ideal contains the other.
They are therefore exactly the minimal primes. Their intersection in the
fibre is zero, their sum is \((F,V)\), and the quotient by their sum is
\(B_\ell\).

Both generators are nonzero, although their product is zero. More
precisely

\[
\operatorname{Ann}(F)=(V),\qquad
\operatorname{Ann}(V)=(F).
\tag{IAR5.4}
\]

This follows immediately from the same unique normal form and the domain
property of \(B_\ell\).

The involution descends to every special fibre, and its exact formulas
are

\[
F^*=V,\quad V^*=F,
\qquad U_p^*=\bar p\,U_p^{-1}\quad(p\ne\ell).
\tag{IAR5.5}
\]

It exchanges the two components and preserves their intersection. In
particular the characteristic-two fibre has the same two-component
description; no prime has been omitted.

For comparison, the inclusion into the bare integral group ring induces

\[
B_\ell[F,V]/(FV)\longrightarrow
k[b_p,b_p^{-1}:p\text{ prime}],
\quad F\longmapsto b_\ell,\quad V\longmapsto0,
\tag{IAR5.6}
\]

and \(U_p\mapsto b_p\) otherwise. The kernel is exactly \((V)\). After
quotienting by \((V)\), the map is the injection of the polynomial ring
in \(F\) into its Laurent localization. Thus adjoining the bare inverse
singles out one of the two fibre components and then inverts its
surviving coordinate. The integral adjoint algebra retains both,
exchanged by its involution. This is a derived description of the map,
not a replacement of the full rational action.

\subsection{IAR6. The actual original-zeta
receiver}\label{iar6.-the-actual-original-zeta-receiver}

Retain the receiving space from IC2:

\[
\mathcal B=\{\text{entire }f:\ f\text{ decreases faster than every inverse power vertically in each bounded real strip}\},
\]

with seminorms

\[
b_{A,M}(f)=\sup_{|\Re s|\le A}(1+|\Im s|)^M|f(s)|.
\]

Let \(\mathscr Z\) be the actual distinct nontrivial zeros of the
original \(\zeta\), with their full multiplicities \(m_\rho\), and
retain

\[
\mathcal I=\{f\in\mathcal B:f^{(j)}(\rho)=0
\text{ for every }\rho\in\mathscr Z, 0\le j<m_\rho\},
\qquad \mathcal Q=\mathcal B/\mathcal I.
\tag{IAR6.1}
\]

The source comparison in IC2 retains its full multiplier
\(s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)/8\), including its endpoint and
trivial-zero comparison. The present coefficient construction does not
modify that comparison or replace the original zeta. It acts on the
already proved quotient.

For \(r>0\), define \(T_r[f]=[r^s f(s)]\), using the real logarithm of
\(r\). The map from the new integral algebra is

\[
\boxed{
\Psi:\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\longrightarrow
\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb C,\mathrm{cont}}(\mathcal Q),
\quad
\Psi(C_{a/b})=b T_{a/b}.}
\tag{IAR6.2}
\]

It is the restriction of the full rational group-algebra action, not a
newly assigned representation. In particular

\[
\Psi(F_p)=T_p,\qquad
\Psi(V_p)=pT_{1/p},\qquad
\Psi(F_p)\Psi(V_p)=pI.
\tag{IAR6.3}
\]

\textbf{Proof of well-definedness and continuity.} Each multiplier is
entire and nowhere zero. Multiplication therefore preserves every
required vanishing order. On representatives,

\[
b_{A,M}(b r^s f)\le b\max(r^A,r^{-A})b_{A,M}(f),
\tag{IAR6.4}
\]

and finite sums satisfy the sum of these bounds. Taking the quotient
gives continuous operators on \(\mathcal Q\). Multiplication of two
basis multipliers gives
\(\operatorname{den}(r)\operatorname{den}(s)(rs)^z\), exactly the image
of (IAR1.2), so the map respects every product and the unit. \(\square\)

The full rational action is recovered by scalar extension of (IAR6.2)
through (IAR2.1). In particular every \(T_{1/p}\) remains available in
the original rational action: it is \(p^{-1}\Psi(V_p)\). The new
statement is that its integral adjoint partner is \(pT_{1/p}\), with the
degree factor retained.

\subsection{IAR7. Every zero jet and its reflected
involution}\label{iar7.-every-zero-jet-and-its-reflected-involution}

For an actual zero \(\rho\), put \(m=m_\rho\). The complete germ
receiver is \(\mathbb C[t]/(t^m)\), with \(s=\rho+t\). Its action is

\[
\begin{aligned}
\Psi(F_p)_\rho
&=p^\rho\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{(\log p)^j}{j!}N_\rho^j,\\
\Psi(V_p)_\rho
&=p^{1-\rho}\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{(-\log p)^j}{j!}N_\rho^j,
\qquad N_\rho^m=0,
\end{aligned}
\tag{IAR7.1}
\]

where \(N_\rho\) is multiplication by \(t\). These are the Taylor
expansions of the actual multipliers, so all nilpotent coefficients are
retained. Their product is \(pI\): the coefficient of \(N_\rho^j\), for
\(j\ge1\), is

\[
p(\log p)^j\sum_{a=0}^j\frac{(-1)^{j-a}}{a!(j-a)!}
=\frac{p(\log p)^j}{j!}(1-1)^j=0,
\]

while the constant coefficient is \(p\). More generally the basis
element \(C_{a/b}\) acts by

\[
b(a/b)^\rho
\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{\bigl(\log(a/b)\bigr)^j}{j!}N_\rho^j.
\tag{IAR7.2}
\]

For \(A=\sum_r c_r b_r\in K\), \(c_r\in\mathbb Q\), denote its
multiplier by \(a_A(s)=\sum_r c_r r^s\). Its algebra involution has
multiplier

\[
a_{A^*}(s)=\sum_r c_r r^{1-s}
=\overline{a_A(1-\bar s)}.
\tag{IAR7.3}
\]

The actual zero divisor is invariant under
\(\rho\mapsto\rho^\#=1-\bar\rho\), with equal multiplicities.
Consequently the full jet comparison is

\[
j_\rho(a_{A^*})
=\sigma\bigl(j_{\rho^\#}(a_A)\bigr),
\qquad
\sigma\left(\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}z_jt^j\right)
=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}(-1)^j\bar z_jt^j.
\tag{IAR7.4}
\]

This formula retains both reflection and the sign on every higher Taylor
coefficient. It is not a restriction to simple zeros or to constant
jets.

For completeness the represented kernel is involution-stable. If
\(\Psi(A)=0\), then \(a_A f\in\mathcal I\) for every \(f\in\mathcal B\).
For any fixed actual \(\rho\), the function \(f(s)=e^{(s-\rho)^2}\)
belongs to \(\mathcal B\) and has value one at \(\rho\). It is an
invertible local germ there, so \(a_A f\) vanishing to order \(m_\rho\)
forces the same vanishing order for \(a_A\). Formula (IAR7.4) proves
that \(a_{A^*}\) has all those orders too, and thus
\(a_{A^*}f\in\mathcal I\) for every test \(f\). Hence \(\Psi(A^*)=0\).
The involution therefore descends to the represented image as an algebra
involution, even without claiming that \(\Psi\) is injective.

\subsection{IAR8. The weighted adjoint identity, including the trace
radical}\label{iar8.-the-weighted-adjoint-identity-including-the-trace-radical}

Retain the actual Weil form

\[
W([f],[g])=\sum_{\rho\in\mathscr Z}m_\rho
\overline{f(\rho^\#)}g(\rho),
\qquad \rho^\#=1-\bar\rho.
\tag{IAR8.1}
\]

Its absolute convergence and Hermitian property are supplied by the
original-zeta receiver. The finite multipliers here are bounded on the
strip containing all these zeros, so the same convergence permits their
insertion. For \(r=a/b>0\),

\[
\begin{aligned}
W(\Psi(C_r)x,y)
&=\sum_\rho m_\rho\,b r^{\overline{\rho^\#}}
\overline{x(\rho^\#)}y(\rho)\\
&=\sum_\rho m_\rho\,b r^{1-\rho}
\overline{x(\rho^\#)}y(\rho)\\
&=\sum_\rho m_\rho\,a r^{-\rho}
\overline{x(\rho^\#)}y(\rho)\\
&=W(x,\Psi(C_{1/r})y).
\end{aligned}
\tag{IAR8.2}
\]

The integer relation \(br=a\) accounts for the coefficient change.
Additivity and real integral coefficients now give, for every
\(A\in\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}\),

\[
\boxed{W(\Psi(A)x,y)=W(x,\Psi(A^*)y).}
\tag{IAR8.3}
\]

In particular \(T_p\) and \(pT_{1/p}\) are adjoint partners for this
exact form, and

\[
W(T_px,T_py)=W(x,pT_{1/p}T_py)=pW(x,y).
\tag{IAR8.4}
\]

This proves the adjoint identity without a positivity assumption. Higher
jets remain present in IAR7 even where this value-only trace form has a
radical. Thus (IAR8.3) is an exact adjoint identity for the specified
algebra involution; it does not assert a unique adjoint for every
continuous endomorphism of a possibly degenerate Hermitian space.

The same distinction matters for finite characteristic. The actual
receiver \(\mathcal Q\) is a complex vector space, so multiplication by
every prime \(\ell\) is invertible. Therefore

\[
\mathcal Q/\ell\mathcal Q=0.
\tag{IAR8.5}
\]

The two nonzero special-fibre components in IAR5 belong to the integral
coefficient algebra. They cannot be obtained as nonzero reductions of
this complex receiving vector space. A nontrivial reduction of a
receiving module would require an actual integral module inside or
before it; none is silently supplied by the coefficient-ring
construction. Formula (IAR8.5) is the exact existing comparison, while
IAR5 retains the nontrivial integral geometry rather than discarding it.

\subsection{IAR9. Proved repair and the next receiving
object}\label{iar9.-proved-repair-and-the-next-receiving-object}

The complete proved diagram is

\[
\begin{array}{ccccc}
\mathbb Z[b_n:n\ge1]&\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow&
\mathcal H_{\mathbb Z}&\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow&
\mathbb Q[\mathbb Q_{>0}^{\times}]\\
&&\downarrow\Psi&&\downarrow\Phi_{\mathbb Q}\\
&&\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb C,\mathrm{cont}}(\mathcal Q)
&=&\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb C,\mathrm{cont}}(\mathcal Q).
\end{array}
\tag{IAR9.1}
\]

The middle ring is the minimal integral adjoint closure of the actual
positive counting action. Its basis contains all rational labels with
their exact denominator coefficients. Its rational scalar extension is
the original full group algebra; its prime special fibres have two
reduced components exchanged by the involution; and its represented
generators retain all original-zeta zero jets and the exact
degree-weighted adjoint.

This resolves the coefficient-domain question for the positive counting
monoid. It does not contradict IC8's different minimum when bare
rational inverses have already been declared integral. The relation
between those two inputs and their resulting rings is proved by the
inclusions and localizations above.

The equation \(F_pV_p=p\), by itself, is not an identification with a
prismatic or Dieudonné object. Here \(F_p,V_p\) are the explicitly
defined elements of a commutative integral subring of a rational group
algebra, with the actual complex receiver and special fibres calculated.
Any comparison with such cohomological theories must preserve those
rings, their coefficient maps, their possible semilinear actions, and
the receiving modules. No such further identification, eigenvalue
integrality conclusion, global positivity result, or RH proof is
asserted in this note.

\section{Integral adjoints, the trace coordinate, and its exact
infinitesimal
fibres}\label{integral-adjoints-the-trace-coordinate-and-its-exact-infinitesimal-fibres}

24 September 2026. Direct derivation AT1--AT6. This continues the linked
task's IC8 integral-adjoint calculation and the present integral return
algebra. The variables here are operators and their algebraic
coordinates, not coordinates or distances assigned to the parityless
supporting datum tau.

\subsection{AT1. The retained integral algebra at one
prime}\label{at1.-the-retained-integral-algebra-at-one-prime}

For each prime \(p\), including two, let \[
B_p=\mathbb Z[F,V]/(FV-p),\qquad F^\star=V,\quad V^\star=F.
\tag{AT1.1}
\] The involution fixes integer scalars. The defining relation is
preserved, so it defines an actual involution of the ring. The
integral-adjoint construction gives the injection \[
B_p\longrightarrow\mathbb Q[\mathbb Q_{>0}^{\times}],\qquad
F\longmapsto b_p,\quad V\longmapsto p b_{1/p}.
\tag{AT1.2}
\] For completeness its injectivity follows by replacing each mixed
monomial \(F^aV^b\) by \(p^{\min(a,b)}F^{a-b}\) or
\(p^{\min(a,b)}V^{b-a}\). Thus every element has a representative
\(c_0+\sum_{j\ge1}c_jF^j+\sum_{j\ge1}d_jV^j\), with finite sums and
integer coefficients. Its image is
\(c_0b_1+\sum c_jb_{p^j}+\sum d_jp^jb_{p^{-j}}\). Distinct positive
rational indices are independent in the group-algebra basis. Vanishing
forces every coefficient to vanish. This proves the asserted injection
and the unique representative. In particular neither \(F\) nor \(V\) has
been made an invertible integral generator by a hidden division by
\(p\).

The all-prime counterpart and its exact minimality are proved in
\nolinkurl{INTEGRAL_ADJOINT_RETURN_ALGEBRA.md}. The ambient rational
group algebra, including its original inverse generators, is retained by
scalar extension. Here the calculation uses the displayed integral
subalgebra and its specified maps.

\subsection{AT2. The exact quadratic presentation and its
discriminant}\label{at2.-the-exact-quadratic-presentation-and-its-discriminant}

Put \(X=F+V\). There is a ring isomorphism \[
\boxed{B_p\simeq\mathbb Z[X,F]/(F^2-XF+p),\qquad V=X-F.}
\tag{AT2.1}
\] The forward substitution gives \(F^2-(F+V)F+p=-FV+p=0\). The reverse
gives \(F(X-F)=p\) by its displayed relation. Each substitution fixes
\(F\) and sends the other generator back to itself, so they are mutually
inverse on all elements. Polynomial division by the monic quadratic
proves that this is a free rank-two module over \(\mathbb Z[X]\), with
basis \(1,F\): existence follows from division, and a remainder of
degree less than two cannot be a nonzero multiple of a monic quadratic.
No inversion of two or of \(p\) is used.

The involution fixes \(X\) and sends \(F\) to \(X-F\). Its exact
discriminant identity is \[
\boxed{(F-V)^2=X^2-4p.}
\tag{AT2.2}
\] Expand both sides using \(FV=p\). Every constant, including four,
remains. This identifies the two-sheeted trace map algebraically while
retaining the original operator coordinates.

The relative differential module makes the ramification calculation
exact: \[
\Omega_{B_p/\mathbb Z[X]}=B_p/(2F-X)\,dF.
\tag{AT2.3}
\] Differentiating the single monic relation over the fixed base gives
precisely \((2F-X)dF=0\). This description includes residue
characteristic two.

\subsection{AT3. Its two characteristic-zero branch fibres are dual
numbers}\label{at3.-its-two-characteristic-zero-branch-fibres-are-dual-numbers}

Retain the integral extension \[
A_p=\mathbb Z[\alpha]/(\alpha^2-p).
\tag{AT3.1}
\] Extend the involution by \(\alpha^\star=\alpha\); this specifies its
action on the new scalar base. For each sign \(\sigma\in\{+1,-1\}\),
take the actual base change of AT2.1 along \(X\mapsto2\sigma\alpha\).
Its coordinate algebra is \[
B_{p,\sigma}=A_p[F]/(F^2-2\sigma\alpha F+p)
=A_p[F]/((F-\sigma\alpha)^2).
\tag{AT3.2}
\] The equalities use \(\alpha^2=p\) and retain the full polynomial
before factoring. The mutually inverse maps \[
\boxed{A_p[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)\longleftrightarrow B_{p,\sigma},
\qquad \varepsilon\longmapsto F-\sigma\alpha,\quad
F\longmapsto\sigma\alpha+\varepsilon}
\tag{AT3.3}
\] give an exact dual-number isomorphism. They also give \[
F=\sigma\alpha+\varepsilon,\qquad
V=\sigma\alpha-\varepsilon,
\quad F+V=2\sigma\alpha,
\quad FV=p-\varepsilon^2=p,
\quad\varepsilon^\star=-\varepsilon.
\tag{AT3.4}
\] The nilpotent \(\varepsilon\) is nonzero: the dual-number ring is
free over \(A_p\) with basis \(1,\varepsilon\), again by division by a
monic polynomial. The trace is exactly constant on this nonreduced fibre
while the two operators change by opposite nonzero first-order terms.
This is the precise vanishing infinitesimal in this construction. It is
not a claim that an original zeta zero has this multiplicity or lies
over one of these two trace values.

There are additional integral features, which must not be hidden by the
characteristic-zero description. The two fibres are defined over
\(A_p\); their base values differ by \(4\alpha\), not by an invertible
constant in general. The relative derivative of the monic quadratic in
AT2.1 is \(2F-X\). Thus its ramification locus is defined by this
derivative, with discriminant \(X^2-4p\); in residue characteristic two
this locus has additional behaviour. AT3.3 is an integral identity valid
even above two, without asserting that the two fibres remain disjoint
there.

The self-adjoint operator quotient has a different exact map: \[
B_p/(F-V)\simeq\mathbb Z[F]/(F^2-p)\simeq A_p.
\tag{AT3.5}
\] Within AT3.3, imposing \(F=V\) imposes \(2\varepsilon=0\), not
automatically \(\varepsilon=0\) over the integral base. After passage to
a real embedding \(\alpha=\sqrt p\), it imposes \(\varepsilon=0\). The
positive cohomological matrix of PRI1 satisfies \(F_p^2=pI\) and has its
adjoint equal to itself, so it receives AT3.5. This retains the
distinction between the nonreduced trace fibre, its reduced real point,
and the integral self-adjoint quotient.

More explicitly, the common quotient after the trace base change is \[
A_p[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2,2\varepsilon)
=A_p\oplus(A_p/2A_p)\varepsilon
\quad\text{as an }A_p\text{-module}.
\tag{AT3.6}
\] The second summand is nonzero since \(A_p\) is free over
\(\mathbb Z\) with basis \(1,\alpha\). To check the comparison without
identifying two scalar variables prematurely, write the original
self-adjoint quotient as \(\mathbb Z[\beta]/(\beta^2-p)\). Its trace
base change also imposes \(2\beta=2\sigma\alpha\). Substitution
\(\varepsilon=\beta-\sigma\alpha\) converts these relations into
\(\varepsilon^2+2\sigma\alpha\varepsilon=0\) and \(2\varepsilon=0\),
exactly the two relations displayed. The retained integral infinitesimal
is therefore nonzero two-torsion; its involution fixes it because
\(-\varepsilon=\varepsilon\) in this quotient.

\subsection{AT4. A faithful indefinite receiver and the exact positive
reduction}\label{at4.-a-faithful-indefinite-receiver-and-the-exact-positive-reduction}

Base change AT3.3 to \(\mathbb R\) through \(\alpha=\sqrt p\). On
\(\mathbb C^2\), retain \[
N=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\0&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad
J=\begin{pmatrix}0&i\\-i&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad
F_\sigma=\sigma\sqrt p\,I+N,\quad
V_\sigma=\sigma\sqrt p\,I-N.
\tag{AT4.1}
\] Then \(N^2=0\) but \(N\ne0\), \(J^*=J\), and \(J\) has eigenvalues
\(+1,-1\). Direct multiplication gives \[
N^*J+JN=0,\qquad
F_\sigma^*J=JV_\sigma,\qquad
F_\sigma V_\sigma=pI,\qquad F_\sigma^*JF_\sigma=pJ.
\tag{AT4.2}
\] The map \(a+b\varepsilon\mapsto aI+bN\) is faithful since its
diagonal and upper-right entry recover \(a\) and \(b\). It therefore
retains the complete infinitesimal algebra with its involution in a
nondegenerate indefinite Hermitian space. The constant trace has not
erased the nilpotent in this receiver.

Conversely, in a representation of this same real dual-number star
algebra by bounded operators on a positive-definite Hilbert space, let
\(E\) be the image of \(\varepsilon\). The relations require \(E^*=-E\)
and \(E^2=0\). For every vector \(v\), \[
\|Ev\|^2=\langle v,E^*Ev\rangle
=\langle v,-E^2v\rangle=0.
\tag{AT4.3}
\] Thus \(E=0\). This proves that every such positive star
representation factors through the explicit quotient \[
\mathbb R[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)\longrightarrow\mathbb R,
\qquad a+b\varepsilon\longmapsto a,
\qquad\ker=(\varepsilon).
\tag{AT4.4}
\] Evaluation on the one-dimensional real Hilbert space is faithful on
the quotient. Consequently \((\varepsilon)\) is exactly the intersection
of kernels of these positive star representations. The indefinite
faithful map and the positive quotient are both constructed; neither
justifies deleting the source infinitesimal from other calculations.

\subsection{AT5. The actual original-zeta receiver and the purity
trace}\label{at5.-the-actual-original-zeta-receiver-and-the-purity-trace}

Use the actual zero-jet quotient \(\mathcal Q\) and its continuous
action \(T_a[F(s)]=[a^sF(s)]\), as proved in the linked task's CD4--CD6
and RZ8. Its multiplier comparison is with the original \(\zeta\),
including the full \(s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)/8\) source factor. On
this quotient there is the ring homomorphism \[
B_p\longrightarrow\operatorname{End}_{\rm cont}(\mathcal Q),
\qquad F\longmapsto T_p,
\quad V\longmapsto pT_{1/p},
\quad X\longmapsto T_p+pT_{1/p}.
\tag{AT5.1}
\] Composition gives \(T_p(pT_{1/p})=pI\), proving the defining
relation. The reflected Weil form makes these two operators adjoints,
exactly as IC8.5 proves; no positive metric is presumed.

For every actual nontrivial zero \(\rho\) with multiplicity \(m_\rho\),
let \(N_\rho^{m_\rho}=0\) be its retained Jordan coordinate. The full
receiving formulas are \[
\begin{split}
F|_\rho&=p^\rho\sum_{j=0}^{m_\rho-1}\frac{(\log p)^j}{j!}N_\rho^j,\\
V|_\rho&=p^{1-\rho}\sum_{j=0}^{m_\rho-1}\frac{(-\log p)^j}{j!}N_\rho^j,\\
X|_\rho&=\sum_{j=0}^{m_\rho-1}
\frac{(\log p)^j}{j!}\bigl(p^\rho+(-1)^jp^{1-\rho}\bigr)N_\rho^j.
\end{split}
\tag{AT5.2}
\] Taylor expansion of the entire multipliers proves each finite sum; no
nilpotent term is dropped. The scalar trace-coordinate value is \[
x_p(\rho)=p^\rho+p^{1-\rho}.
\tag{AT5.3}
\] The following characterization holds for every complex \(s\), hence
also on the entire actual-zero set: \[
\boxed{\Re s=\tfrac12
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
p^s+p^{1-s}\in[-2\sqrt p,2\sqrt p]\subset\mathbb R.}
\tag{AT5.4}
\] To prove the forward direction write \(s=1/2+it\), giving the
retained value \(2\sqrt p\cos(t\log p)\). For the reverse direction put
\(x=p^s+p^{1-s}\) in the real interval. Both \(p^s\) and \(p^{1-s}\) are
roots of \(Y^2-xY+p\). Its roots are \((x\pm i\sqrt{4p-x^2})/2\),
including the coincident endpoints, and both have squared modulus
\((x^2+4p-x^2)/4=p\). Therefore \(|p^s|^2=p^{2\Re s}=p\). Since \(p>1\),
taking real logarithms proves the equivalence.

Equivalently the character \(F\mapsto p^s,V\mapsto p^{1-s}\) preserves
\(\star\) as complex conjugation exactly on that same line: the required
equality is \(p^{1-s}=\overline{p^s}\), which holds there; its absolute
values imply \(1-\Re s=\Re s\) in the converse direction. The full phase
\(\Im s\log p\) remains. Thus the integral trace algebra supplies an
exact place to test purity of the actual zero characters, rather than
identifying an integer degree with an unknown algebraic eigenvalue.

AT5.4 is an exact identity for all complex inputs. It is not a finite
interval computation of zeros, an established bound for their trace
values, or an RH conclusion. The displayed trace interval is the
mathematical condition whose global proof is sought.

The scalar trace observation has the following exact fibre for any
\(s,t\in\mathbb C\): \[
x_p(t)=x_p(s)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
t=s+\frac{2\pi i k}{\log p}\ \text{or}\
t=1-s+\frac{2\pi i k}{\log p},\qquad k\in\mathbb Z.
\tag{AT5.5}
\] Both exponentials are roots of the same quadratic precisely when
\(p^t\) is either \(p^s\) or \(p^{1-s}\). The exponential with real
logarithm has exactly the displayed periods, proving both directions.
Thus the full receiver retains its two ordered phase factors, while
their sum identifies the two roots; the involution and exponential
periods are recorded by this observation map.

There is also an exact check of when the actual jet factors through the
constant-trace fibre. At a coincident-root value, put
\(a=p^s=p^{1-s}=\sigma\sqrt p\), \(q=\log p\), and let \(N\) be the
retained nilpotent local coordinate. Formula AT5.2 gives \[
X-2aI=aq^2N^2\left(I+\frac{q^2}{12}N^2+\frac{q^4}{360}N^4+\cdots\right).
\tag{AT5.6}
\] The sum stops when the powers of \(N\) vanish. Its parenthesized
factor is invertible: it is \(I+K\) with \(K\) nilpotent, whose inverse
is the finite geometric series \(I-K+K^2-\cdots\). Also \(aq^2\ne0\).
Consequently the trace is constant exactly when \(N^2=0\). On a nonzero
first-order jet the exact map from AT3.3 after complex scalar extension
is \(\varepsilon\mapsto aqN\), with inverse
\(N\mapsto\varepsilon/(aq)\); it sends \(F=a+\varepsilon\) and
\(V=a-\varepsilon\) to the actual multiplier polynomials. For higher
jets the displayed second-order term survives, and the full jet does not
factor through that same constant-trace fibre. No existence or
multiplicity of an actual zeta zero at these special values is assumed.

\subsection{AT6. Relation to the earlier infinitesimal and integral
questions}\label{at6.-relation-to-the-earlier-infinitesimal-and-integral-questions}

The integral adjoint algebra closes the weighted-adjoint operation
without making every bare inverse integral. Its trace map then has the
exact dual-number fibres AT3.3. The infinitesimal disappears under the
trace while surviving faithfully in AT4.1, and positive star
representations kill it through the explicit quotient AT4.4. These are
completed calculations on specified objects, with their connecting maps.

For the original-zeta quotient, the exact receiving map is AT5.1--AT5.2.
No identification of its unknown zero locations with the branch fibres
is made. The full receiver retains both phase factors and every
nilpotent coefficient; the scalar trace has the exact fibres AT5.5. The
criterion AT5.4 retains the original prime norm, and proving it on every
actual zero remains the purity problem. The nilpotent computation gives
a usable representation and its precise positive reduction, not a sign
change of the original Weil form by declaration.

\section{Global joint traces and the complete original-zeta
jets}\label{global-joint-traces-and-the-complete-original-zeta-jets}

24 September 2026. Independent derivation. Proof locators JTR0--JTR8.

This note proves exactly which information the ordered counting-adjoint
operators and their trace coordinates retain, globally and at every
actual nontrivial zero of the original zeta. All statements concern the
complete indicated sets or spaces. No numerical range of zeros is
tested. The supporting datum
\(\tau\langle Z_1;\text{no intrinsic }Z_2\rangle\) receives no
arithmetic coordinate, addition, or metric.

\subsection{JTR0. Retained receiving objects and
conventions}\label{jtr0.-retained-receiving-objects-and-conventions}

The source dependencies are
\nolinkurl{ADJOINT_TRACE_AND_DUAL_FIBRES.md}, AT1--AT6, read in full,
and \nolinkurl{INTEGRAL_ADJOINT_RETURN_ALGEBRA.md}, IAR6--IAR8,
independently derived and proved before this calculation. The latter
receives the original quotient and weighted Weil adjoint from the linked
task's IC2 and IC8. No additional source discovery or new cohomological
identification is used here.

For each prime \(p\), retain its positive real logarithm \(q_p=\log p\),
and define entire functions on the original complex \(s\)-plane:

\[
F_p(s)=p^s=e^{q_ps},\qquad
V_p(s)=p^{1-s}=p e^{-q_ps},\qquad
x_p(s)=F_p(s)+V_p(s).
\tag{JTR0.1}
\]

The ordered pair is retained; it is not replaced by its sum. Its product
is exactly \(p\). On the actual zero-jet quotient, these are the
multipliers of \(T_p\), \(pT_{1/p}\), and their sum.

The holomorphic reflection and the anti-holomorphic Weil reflection are
respectively

\[
j(s)=1-s,\qquad \iota(s)=1-\bar s.
\tag{JTR0.2}
\]

They must not be interchanged: the trace functions satisfy

\[
x_p(j(s))=x_p(s),\qquad
x_p(\iota(s))=\overline{x_p(s)}.
\tag{JTR0.3}
\]

The first is a holomorphic two-root identification; the second includes
conjugation. Both follow directly from (JTR0.1) and the real logarithm
convention.

\subsection{JTR1. The elementary independence of distinct prime
periods}\label{jtr1.-the-elementary-independence-of-distinct-prime-periods}

For distinct primes \(p,q\),

\[
\frac{\log p}{\log q}\notin\mathbb Q.
\tag{JTR1.1}
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Both logarithms are positive. A rational ratio would be
\(a/b\) for positive integers \(a,b\), so \(b\log p=a\log q\), hence
\(p^b=q^a\). Divisibility by the distinct primes contradicts unique
prime factorization. \(\square\)

In particular, for integers \(k,\ell\),

\[
\frac{k}{\log p}=\frac{\ell}{\log q}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad k=\ell=0.
\tag{JTR1.2}
\]

If one integer is zero this is immediate; otherwise it would give a
rational ratio in (JTR1.1). This exact relation is the only independence
fact required below. No unproved algebraic independence of logarithms
enters.

\subsection{JTR2. Two complete ordered pairs separate every complex
point}\label{jtr2.-two-complete-ordered-pairs-separate-every-complex-point}

For a single prime, the precise fibre of the complete ordered pair is

\[
(F_p(t),V_p(t))=(F_p(s),V_p(s))
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
t-s\in\frac{2\pi i}{\log p}\mathbb Z.
\tag{JTR2.1}
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Equality of the first coordinates says
\(e^{q_p(t-s)}=1\), equivalent to \(q_p(t-s)=2\pi i k\) for an integer
\(k\). Conversely such a period preserves the first coordinate and also
its reciprocal times \(p\), the second coordinate. \(\square\)

Consequently the entire map

\[
\mathcal F_{p,q}:\mathbb C\longrightarrow(\mathbb C^\times)^4,
\qquad s\longmapsto(F_p(s),V_p(s),F_q(s),V_q(s))
\tag{JTR2.2}
\]

is injective for any two distinct primes. Equality at two points gives

\[
t-s=\frac{2\pi i k}{\log p}
=\frac{2\pi i\ell}{\log q},
\]

and JTR1 forces both integers to be zero. This proof covers every
complex \(s,t\). It does not merely distinguish points in a selected
strip or bounded height range.

The differential of even one first coordinate is \(F_p'(s)=q_p p^s\ne0\)
everywhere. Thus there is also no local loss of an infinitesimal
coordinate in the full pair. Its global one-prime periodic ambiguity and
its local invertibility are different properties, with the exact fibre
(JTR2.1) relating them.

\subsection{JTR3. Three traces separate precisely the reflection
orbits}\label{jtr3.-three-traces-separate-precisely-the-reflection-orbits}

For a single prime the complete trace fibre is

\[
\boxed{
x_p(t)=x_p(s)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
t=s+\frac{2\pi i k}{\log p}
\ \text{or}\ 
t=1-s+\frac{2\pi i k}{\log p},
\quad k\in\mathbb Z.}
\tag{JTR3.1}
\]

\textbf{Proof.} Both \(F_p(s)\) and \(V_p(s)\) are roots of
\(Y^2-x_p(s)Y+p\). If the trace values agree, the same equation has
\(F_p(t)\) as a root. It follows that \(F_p(t)=F_p(s)\) or
\(F_p(t)=V_p(s)\). Their exponential fibres give exactly the two
alternatives. Each alternative conversely gives the same trace by
substitution. Repeated roots cause no exception: then the two
alternatives coincide modulo their displayed period. \(\square\)

\textbf{Theorem.} For any three distinct primes \(p,q,r\),

\[
\boxed{
(x_p(t),x_q(t),x_r(t))=(x_p(s),x_q(s),x_r(s))
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad t=s\text{ or }t=1-s.}
\tag{JTR3.2}
\]

\textbf{Proof.} At each of the three primes, choose one of the valid
alternatives in (JTR3.1). There are two types and three choices, so two
distinct primes have the same type. If they both have the first type,
their two periods give \(t-s=2\pi i k/\log p=2\pi i\ell/\log q\); JTR1
gives \(t=s\). If both have the second type, apply the same argument to
\(t-(1-s)\) to obtain \(t=1-s\). The converse follows from (JTR0.3).
This argument still applies at coincident roots, because either of their
valid types may be chosen. \(\square\)

Equivalently the trace map factors through \(s\mapsto\{s,1-s\}\) and is
injective on that orbit set. It does not pick one of the two points of
an orbit. The two full ordered pairs in JTR2 retain precisely the branch
information that this trace observation identifies.

Two traces alone can have further coincidences; the following exact
family locates them rather than dropping them. For distinct primes
\(p,q\) and nonzero integers \(k,\ell\), put

\[
\begin{aligned}
s&=\frac12+\pi i\left(\frac{\ell}{\log q}-\frac{k}{\log p}\right),\\
t&=\frac12+\pi i\left(\frac{\ell}{\log q}+\frac{k}{\log p}\right).
\end{aligned}
\tag{JTR3.3}
\]

Then \(t=s+2\pi i k/\log p\) and \(t=1-s+2\pi i\ell/\log q\), so both
traces agree. Neither \(t=s\) nor \(t=1-s\) holds because the two
integers are nonzero. This is an infinite symbolic family, not a
numerical search or a claim about actual zeta zeros.

More generally, any coincidence of two traces outside the two reflection
alternatives must use different types for the two primes in (JTR3.1). In
that case taking real parts gives both \(\Re t=\Re s\) and
\(\Re t=1-\Re s\), so both points lie on \(\Re s=1/2\). Thus the extra
ambiguity of two traces has an exact location. The third trace removes
it globally by the proved orientation argument.

\subsection{JTR4. The only common ramification point of two trace
coordinates}\label{jtr4.-the-only-common-ramification-point-of-two-trace-coordinates}

Differentiate the complete original trace:

\[
x_p'(s)=q_p\bigl(p^s-p^{1-s}\bigr).
\tag{JTR4.1}
\]

Since \(q_p>0\), it vanishes exactly when \(p^{2s-1}=1\). Therefore

\[
x_p'(s)=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
s=\frac12+\frac{\pi i k}{\log p},\quad k\in\mathbb Z.
\tag{JTR4.2}
\]

For any distinct primes \(p,q\), simultaneous vanishing requires
\(k/\log p=\ell/\log q\), so JTR1 gives

\[
\boxed{x_p'(s)=x_q'(s)=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad s=\frac12.}
\tag{JTR4.3}
\]

This is the unique common ramification point of the joint local trace
observation. It is a statement about the actual functions on the
original \(s\)-plane, without assigning a coordinate to the supporting
point tau.

\subsection{JTR5. The original zeta is nonzero at the common
ramification
point}\label{jtr5.-the-original-zeta-is-nonzero-at-the-common-ramification-point}

Retain the exact alternating-series factor, rather than substituting
another function for the original zeta. On \(\Re s>0\), define

\[
\eta(s)=\sum_{n\ge1}(-1)^{n-1}n^{-s}.
\tag{JTR5.1}
\]

This series defines a holomorphic function there. For a direct
convergence proof, the partial sums of the alternating coefficients of
any tail have absolute value at most one. Summation by parts and

\[
|n^{-s}-(n+1)^{-s}|
\le |s|\int_n^{n+1}x^{-\Re s-1}\,dx
\]

bound the infinite tail after \(N\), on any compact set with
\(\Re s\ge\delta>0\) and \(|s|\le C\), by \((C/\delta)(N+1)^{-\delta}\).
A terminal finite-sum boundary tends to zero. Thus the convergence is
locally uniform, and the terms are holomorphic.

On the half-plane of absolute convergence, separate the original odd and
even terms:

\[
\begin{aligned}
\eta(s)
&=\sum_{n\ge1}n^{-s}-2\sum_{n\ge1}(2n)^{-s}\\
&=\bigl(1-2^{1-s}\bigr)\zeta(s),\qquad \Re s>1.
\end{aligned}
\tag{JTR5.2}
\]

The same equality holds meromorphically on \(\Re s>0\) by uniqueness of
continuation of the original zeta. Its entire multiplier \(1-2^{1-s}\)
is retained; this is not an assertion that \(\eta\) and \(\zeta\) have
the same global zero or pole divisor. At \(s=1/2\) the multiplier is
\(1-\sqrt2\ne0\), so inversion at that specific point is valid.

Pair consecutive terms at this real argument. Every pair is positive,
and the convergence just proved permits the consecutive pairing:

\[
\eta(1/2)
=\sum_{n\ge1}\left((2n-1)^{-1/2}-(2n)^{-1/2}\right)
\ge 1-2^{-1/2}>0.
\tag{JTR5.3}
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{\zeta(1/2)=\frac{\eta(1/2)}{1-\sqrt2}<0,}
\tag{JTR5.4}
\]

in particular it is not zero. This proof uses the original factor and no
assumption about the nontrivial zeros. The same argument, with exponent
any real \(x\in(0,1)\), proves \(\zeta(x)<0\): the paired numerator is
positive and \(1-2^{1-x}<0\). No general division by the factor at its
other zeros is made.

Combining this with JTR4, every actual nontrivial zero \(\rho\) has at
least one unramified trace coordinate among the fixed primes \(2\) and
\(3\):

\[
\boxed{\text{For every actual }\rho,
\quad x_2'(\rho)\ne0\text{ or }x_3'(\rho)\ne0.}
\tag{JTR5.5}
\]

The assertion is global on the entire actual-zero set and retains all
multiplicities.

\subsection{JTR6. Exact recovery of every finite zero
jet}\label{jtr6.-exact-recovery-of-every-finite-zero-jet}

Let \(\rho\) be an actual nontrivial zero of multiplicity \(m=m_\rho\).
Its retained local algebra is

\[
A_\rho=\mathbb C[h]/(h^m),\qquad s=\rho+h.
\tag{JTR6.1}
\]

This is the full germ receiver, not only a formal upper bound for its
possible values. The quotient \(\mathcal Q=\mathcal B/\mathcal I\) has
the well-defined map \(j_\rho[f]=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}f^{(j)}(\rho)h^j/j!\).
For any desired polynomial \(q(h)\) of degree less than \(m\), let
\(P(h)\) be the truncation of \(e^{-h^2}q(h)\) modulo \(h^m\). Then

\[
f(s)=e^{(s-\rho)^2}P(s-\rho)
\]

is entire and rapidly decreasing vertically on every bounded real strip,
and \(j_\rho[f]=q(h)\). Thus the receiver onto \(A_\rho\) is surjective.
The joint map over all actual zeros is injective on \(\mathcal Q\),
because its kernel is exactly the defining ideal \(\mathcal I\); this
does not assert unrestricted surjectivity onto an infinite product.

Choose \(p\in\{2,3\}\) for which JTR5.5 gives \(x_p'(\rho)\ne0\). Set

\[
u_p(h)=x_p(\rho+h)-x_p(\rho)
=\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}a_jh^j,
\]

\[
a_j=\frac{q_p^j}{j!}
\bigl(p^\rho+(-1)^jp^{1-\rho}\bigr),
\qquad a_1\ne0.
\tag{JTR6.2}
\]

All terms are evaluated in \(A_\rho\); this is the actual trace operator
from AT5.2 with its scalar constant subtracted, not an assumed formal
surrogate.

\textbf{Theorem.} The substitution map

\[
\boxed{\mathbb C[u]/(u^m)\longrightarrow A_\rho,
\qquad u\longmapsto u_p(h),}
\tag{JTR6.3}
\]

is an algebra isomorphism, with a fully specified truncated inverse.

\textbf{Proof.} For each \(0\le j<m\), the polynomial \(u_p(h)^j\) has
first nonzero term \(a_1^j h^j\), with the convention \(j=0\) giving
one. Relative to the bases \(1,u,\ldots,u^{m-1}\) and
\(1,h,\ldots,h^{m-1}\), the substitution matrix is triangular with
nonzero diagonal

\[
1,a_1,a_1^2,\ldots,a_1^{m-1}.
\]

It is invertible. Substitution preserves products and unit, so this is
an algebra isomorphism.

For its exact inverse, put \(h=g(u)=\sum_{n=1}^{m-1}b_nu^n\). For
\(m\ge2\), define recursively

\[
b_1=a_1^{-1},
\qquad
b_n=-a_1^{-1}
\sum_{k=2}^n a_k
\sum_{\substack{j_1,\ldots,j_k\ge1\\j_1+\cdots+j_k=n}}
b_{j_1}\cdots b_{j_k}
\quad(2\le n<m).
\tag{JTR6.4}
\]

Every index in the inner sum is at most \(n-1\), so the recursion uses
only coefficients already defined. The coefficient of \(u^n\) in
\(u_p(g(u))\) is \(a_1b_n\) plus precisely the sum cancelled by this
formula. Thus \(u_p(g(u))=u\) modulo \(u^m\). The other composition is
the identity as well: for nilpotent inputs \(a,b\), the difference
\(u_p(a)-u_p(b)\) equals \((a-b)\) times a polynomial with constant term
\(a_1\), hence a unit. Applying this to the two compositions proves
\(g(u_p(h))=h\) modulo \(h^m\). For \(m=1\), both algebras are just
\(\mathbb C\), and the inverse contains no nonconstant term. \(\square\)

In operator form, with \(N_\rho\) multiplication by \(h\), this is the
exact identity

\[
\boxed{N_\rho=g\bigl(X_p|_\rho-x_p(\rho)I\bigr).}
\tag{JTR6.5}
\]

The trace operator therefore retains the entire local multiplicity
algebra, including every nilpotent coefficient, once an unramified
coordinate has been selected. The pair of primes \(2,3\) supplies such a
selection at every actual zero; there is no common first-order loss on
the actual-zero set.

There remains a precise global branch distinction. The trace functions
identify \(\rho\) and \(1-\rho\). Their local comparison sends the
coordinate \(h\) at one point to \(-h\) at the other:

\[
x_p(1-\rho+h)=x_p(\rho-h).
\tag{JTR6.6}
\]

Thus trace data retain full jets on each chosen branch, while their
global observation identifies the two reflected branches by this
explicit isomorphism. The complete ordered pairs distinguish the
branches by JTR2. This does not assert that scalar trace values
independently prescribe arbitrary data at both identified points.

\subsection{JTR7. The completed local observation at the common
point}\label{jtr7.-the-completed-local-observation-at-the-common-point}

Although it is not a zero of the original zeta, the unique common
ramification point has an exact local observation algebra. Put
\(s=1/2+h\) and work in the complete local ring \(\mathbb C[[h]]\). Then

\[
\begin{aligned}
F_p&=\sqrt p\,e^{q_ph},\\
V_p&=\sqrt p\,e^{-q_ph},\\
u_p:=x_p-2\sqrt p
&=2\sqrt p\sum_{j\ge1}\frac{q_p^{2j}}{(2j)!}h^{2j}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{JTR7.1}
\]

All constants and powers of \(\log p\) are retained. Define the
completed trace-observation algebra to be the closure, in the
\((h)\)-adic topology, of the \(\mathbb C\)-subalgebra generated by the
\(u_p\) for all primes.

\textbf{Theorem.} This completed observation algebra is exactly

\[
\boxed{\mathbb C[[h^2]]\ \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\ \mathbb C[[h]].}
\tag{JTR7.2}
\]

In fact the closure of the subalgebra from any one prime already equals
\(\mathbb C[[h^2]]\).

\textbf{Proof.} Each \(u_p\) is even, so every polynomial in these
elements and every formal limit has only even powers of \(h\). This
proves containment in \(\mathbb C[[h^2]]\), a closed subalgebra.
Conversely put \(v=h^2\). For each prime,

\[
u_p=\sqrt p\,q_p^2v
+2\sqrt p\sum_{j\ge2}\frac{q_p^{2j}}{(2j)!}v^j.
\tag{JTR7.3}
\]

Its linear coefficient in \(v\) is nonzero. The coefficient recursion in
JTR6, now continued at every degree, constructs a unique inverse formal
series \(v=g_p(u_p)\). Every coefficient requires only finitely many
earlier coefficients, so this defines a formal inverse without an
analytic convergence assumption. Hence \(v\) is in the completed algebra
generated by \(u_p\), as is every series in \(v\). This proves equality.
\(\square\)

The holomorphic sign involution is

\[
j^*f(h)=f(-h).
\]

Its fixed subalgebra is exactly \(\mathbb C[[h^2]]\), since equality of
coefficients forces every odd coefficient to vanish. More completely,

\[
\mathbb C[[h]]
=\mathbb C[[h^2]]\ \oplus\ h\mathbb C[[h^2]],
\tag{JTR7.4}
\]

with the involution acting by \(+1\) and \(-1\) on the two summands. The
two projections are exactly \((f(h)+f(-h))/2\) and \((f(h)-f(-h))/2\).
This is a decomposition of the local power-series ring, not a parity
assignment to the supporting datum tau.

The weighted adjoint involution on complex germs includes conjugation as
well:

\[
f(h)=\sum_j a_jh^j
\quad\longmapsto\quad
\sum_j(-1)^j\bar a_jh^j.
\tag{JTR7.5}
\]

It exchanges \(F_p,V_p\) and fixes the real-coefficient trace. Its real
fixed set should not be confused with the complex fixed subalgebra of
the holomorphic sign involution in (JTR7.4).

At any finite jet length \(m\), the trace image is the exact even
subalgebra

\[
\mathbb C[v]/(v^{\lceil m/2\rceil})
\ \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\ 
\mathbb C[h]/(h^m),\qquad v\longmapsto h^2.
\tag{JTR7.6}
\]

Its kernel before dividing by the displayed power is exactly
\((v^{\lceil m/2\rceil})\), because \(h^{2j}\) is nonzero precisely for
\(2j<m\). This records the entire finite loss at the exceptional point,
not only its first-order term.

The odd information is preserved by the full pair. For example

\[
F_p-V_p=2\sqrt p\sum_{j\ge0}
\frac{q_p^{2j+1}}{(2j+1)!}h^{2j+1}
\tag{JTR7.7}
\]

has a nonzero linear coefficient. Even the first coordinate gives the
exact formal inverse

\[
\boxed{h=\frac1{q_p}
\sum_{j\ge1}\frac{(-1)^{j+1}}j
\left(\frac{F_p-\sqrt p}{\sqrt p}\right)^j.}
\tag{JTR7.8}
\]

This is the formal logarithm of the original exponential, with its
constant \(\sqrt p\) and factor \(q_p\) explicitly retained. It is well
defined because its input has zero constant term. It proves that
retaining the full operator coordinate recovers \(\mathbb C[[h]]\), and
reduction modulo \(h^m\) gives its finite-jet version.

\subsection{JTR8. Consequence and exact limits of the joint
observation}\label{jtr8.-consequence-and-exact-limits-of-the-joint-observation}

The global and local maps proved here are:

\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
\mathbb C&\xrightarrow{\mathcal F_{2,3}}&
(\mathbb C^\times)^4,
\qquad\text{injective with all local jets retained};\\
\mathbb C/\{s\sim1-s\}&\xrightarrow{(x_2,x_3,x_5)}&
\mathbb C^3,
\qquad\text{injective on reflection orbits};\\
\mathbb C[h]/(h^{m_\rho})&\longleftrightarrow&
\mathbb C[u_p]/(u_p^{m_\rho}),
\quad p\in\{2,3\}\text{ chosen with }x_p'(\rho)\ne0,
\end{array}
\tag{JTR8.1}
\]

where the third line applies to every actual original-zeta zero and has
the exact inverse coefficients (JTR6.4). These statements do not replace
the full arithmetic spectrum by three primes; they prove that these
particular coordinates separate the indicated parameters within the
complete receiving construction. Every prime and its original return
data remain in that construction.

At \(s=1/2\), the complete trace observation is the even subalgebra and
the full pair retains the odd part. The original zeta is not zero there,
so this common ramification does not erase a jet of any actual
nontrivial zero. Individual trace coordinates may ramify at other
points; another coordinate among the two fixed primes repairs that loss
by the proved local isomorphism.

The results provide global point separation and exact preservation of
the finite multiplicity algebras. They do not assert that every
arbitrary infinite list of jets is represented by a test function, nor
that trace values choose a branch within a reflected orbit. They supply
the precise maps that retain or identify those data.

Finally, the purity criterion from AT5 remains an additional
mathematical statement to establish on the actual spectrum:

\[
x_p(\rho)\in[-2\sqrt p,2\sqrt p]\subset\mathbb R.
\]

The joint observation theorems do not assume this inclusion or infer it
merely from injectivity. They show that its target contains faithful
local arithmetic data and a globally specified reflection
identification, while preserving the ordered operator pairs and all
phases necessary for the subsequent original Weil calculation. No global
positivity theorem or RH conclusion is claimed.

\section{Retaining support at every zero
coefficient}\label{retaining-support-at-every-zero-coefficient}

24 September 2026. ZR1-ZR5. This corrects the support-retention wording
of M9.7 and S7 while retaining their linear isomorphisms and synthesis
theorem. The correction was supplied by the receiving task in
\nolinkurl{ITEM26_GLOBAL_RECEIVERS_INTEGRATION.md}, I26.10; its complete argument
is reproduced and propagated here. The record set below is the
programme's existing set of labels, not an arithmetic value assigned to
an unsupported element.

\subsection{ZR1. Exact domain of the linear
statement}\label{zr1.-exact-domain-of-the-linear-statement}

Let \(\mathcal Q=\mathcal B/\mathcal I\) be the actual original-zeta
quotient, and let \(J:\mathcal Q\to\prod_\rho\mathbb C^{m_\rho}\) be the
injective map of all zero jets proved in S7. Let \(\mathcal L\) be the
retained set of support records. Write \(e_\ell\) for the basis of the
free vector space \(\mathbb C^{(\mathcal L)}\). The linear comparison \[
J\otimes\mathrm{id}:\mathcal Q\otimes\mathbb C^{(\mathcal L)}
\longrightarrow (\prod_\rho\mathbb C^{m_\rho})\otimes\mathbb C^{(\mathcal L)}
\tag{ZR1.1}
\] is injective: every tensor has a finite expansion
\(\sum q_\ell\otimes e_\ell\), and the coordinate functional at each
\(e_\ell\) makes a zero image imply \(Jq_\ell=0\), hence \(q_\ell=0\).
This proves precisely the linear assertion. However, \[
0_{\mathcal Q}\otimes e_\ell=0_{\mathcal Q}\otimes e_{\ell'}=0
\tag{ZR1.2}
\] for every pair of records. The passage from a labelled coefficient to
a tensor has already identified its zero sections. Injectivity of ZR1.1
does not undo that earlier map.

\subsection{ZR2. Exact record-retaining quotient and
observation}\label{zr2.-exact-record-retaining-quotient-and-observation}

Retain the disjoint family \[
\widehat{\mathcal Q}=\coprod_{\ell\in\mathcal L}\{\ell\}\times\mathcal Q.
\tag{ZR2.1}
\] It is equivalently the quotient of \(\mathcal L\times\mathcal B\) by
\((\ell,f)\sim(\ell',g)\) exactly when \(\ell=\ell'\) and
\(f-g\in\mathcal I\). This is an equivalence relation since
\(\mathcal I\) is a vector subspace. The map to ZR2.1 is
\((\ell,f)\mapsto(\ell,[f])\); its fibres are exactly that relation,
proving the comparison. All subtractions occur in the coefficient
functions. No addition is defined on \(\mathcal L\).

The complete observation is \[
\widehat J(\ell,q)=(\ell,Jq).
\tag{ZR2.2}
\] If its two values agree, their first coordinates give equal records
and its second coordinates give equal coefficient classes by injectivity
of \(J\). Thus \(\widehat J\) is injective. The fibre of the
coefficient-only zero observation is exactly \[
\coprod_{\ell\in\mathcal L}\{(\ell,0_{\mathcal Q})\}.
\tag{ZR2.3}
\] It contains every original record, including those that must
distinguish absence from a supported zero.

\subsection{ZR3. The map to the previous tensors, with its full
fibres}\label{zr3.-the-map-to-the-previous-tensors-with-its-full-fibres}

Define \[
c:\widehat{\mathcal Q}\longrightarrow
\mathcal Q\otimes\mathbb C^{(\mathcal L)},\qquad
c(\ell,q)=q\otimes e_\ell.
\tag{ZR3.1}
\] Its zero fibre is exactly ZR2.3, because the coordinate functional at
\(\ell\) recovers \(q\). If its common output is nonzero, equality of
two images with different labels is impossible: the coordinate
functional at the first label would give \(q=0\). Equality with the same
label gives equal coefficients. Thus every nonzero fibre in its image is
a singleton; tensors with several nonzero label coordinates are outside
its image. This states every fibre without asserting that the map is
surjective. The square with \(\widehat J\), \(c\), and
\(J\otimes\mathrm{id}\) commutes by evaluation on \((\ell,q)\).

\subsection{ZR4. Arithmetic action, projectors, and the full
pairing}\label{zr4.-arithmetic-action-projectors-and-the-full-pairing}

Every existing coefficient operator \(A:\mathcal Q\to\mathcal Q\) lifts
as \[
\widehat A(\ell,q)=(\ell,Aq).
\tag{ZR4.1}
\] It satisfies \(\widehat A\widehat B=\widehat{AB}\),
\(\widehat{\mathrm{id}}=\mathrm{id}\), and
\(c\widehat A=(A\otimes\mathrm{id})c\) by direct substitution. This
applies to all actual prime operators, every IAR coefficient operator,
and every zero projector. Addition of operators remains fibrewise
addition on \(\mathcal Q\); it is not an operation on the primitive
records. An operator sending a coefficient to zero sends \((\ell,q)\) to
\((\ell,0)\), retaining its record.

The full Weil pairing has the record-preserving receiver \[
((\ell,x),(\ell',y))\longmapsto(\ell,\ell',W(x,y)).
\tag{ZR4.2}
\] Projection to the last coordinate is exactly the previous form, with
all its endpoint, Gamma, prime and original-zero contributions
unchanged. Its weighted-adjoint identity is therefore the same equality
of last coordinates with the same two retained labels. A vanishing form
value deletes neither input record. No sign or positivity is inferred
from this recording map.

\subsection{ZR5. The two coefficient sections at
zero}\label{zr5.-the-two-coefficient-sections-at-zero}

The formal coefficient algebra \(C_{\rm rec}\) of M9.7 has independent
basis elements \(U=[\tau]\) and \(f=[1]\). Its two linear sections
\(q\mapsto q\otimes U\) and \(q\mapsto q\otimes f\) are distinct maps,
but both take zero to zero. Their linear difference is injective, as
M9.7 proves; that still does not distinguish which zero section was
selected.

To retain that already specified selection, use the record set
\(\mathcal L\times\{U,f\}\) and apply ZR2. The maps from the two
selected families to the previous coefficient receiver are exactly \[
(\ell,U,q)\longmapsto q\otimes U\otimes e_\ell,
\qquad
(\ell,f,q)\longmapsto q\otimes f\otimes e_\ell.
\tag{ZR5.1}
\] For nonzero coefficients their images are distinct by the independent
basis coordinates. At zero the original records remain distinct in the
domain and their comparison images coincide. This proves both the
retained distinction and the specific loss under linear observation. It
assigns no source sum, difference, parity or numerical value to
\(\tau\).

\section{Complete receiving-source
dependencies}\label{complete-receiving-source-dependencies}

\section{Global synthesis for the original-zeta Mellin
quotient}\label{global-synthesis-for-the-original-zeta-mellin-quotient}

Independent derivation, 24 September 2026. This note proves the equality
left open in M9 of \nolinkurl{PRIME_MONODROMY_STACKED_HISTORY.md}. It
uses no RH assumption, no simplicity assumption, no bounded numerical
experiment, and no assumed lower bound for zeta inside the critical
strip. Addition at source tau is not used. The user's state-layer
notation is unchanged.

\subsection{Source identity and actual
reading}\label{source-identity-and-actual-reading}

The existing machine-readable corpus index was queried before consulting
further literature:
\nolinkurl{F:/user/Documents/Papors/Chatnotes/Zeta-Function-Foundation/generated/research_canonical_index.sqlite},
table \texttt{canonical\_units}, for spectral/zeta, spectral synthesis,
